# 4. Phase A — prove real budget-gated ACMS → Bridge → Hermes dispatch

This is the exact next action from the previous handoff.

## A1. Configure live Bridge targets

On CT122:

1. inspect current deployment/env conventions;
2. set `ACMS_BRIDGE_TARGETS_JSON` outside Git;
3. include only the intended test worker (`acms-worker-001` / VM124);
4. validate without printing secrets;
5. restart/redeploy only the required ACMS service;
6. verify `/health`, `/version`, and existing smoke checks.

Do not put Bridge credentials into Git, logs, screenshots, or handoff.

## A2. Create one bounded real Work Item

Use a harmless deterministic task.

Requirements:

```text
clear Work ID
clear acceptance criteria
small soft/hard budget
no destructive operations
no sensitive/customer data
no infrastructure change
```

## A3. Dispatch through the authoritative path

Use:

```text
POST /api/v1/dispatch/work/{work_item_id}
```

Prove:

```text
Work
→ budget execution-check
→ execution task
→ Bridge
→ Hermes
→ model execution
→ result
→ handoff/checkpoint
→ cost attribution
→ economics records
→ semantic events
```

## A4. Validate idempotency/accounting

Verify:

```text
retry does not create duplicate execution
model/provider/session linkage correct
token/cost records correlated
normal successful execution does not create spurious Attention
SSE reflects meaningful state changes
```

Use tests/mocks for hard-budget denial if a production denial probe would be wasteful.

## A5. Record proof

Capture:

```text
Work ID
Assignment ID
Execution Session ID
A2A/Bridge task ID
model/provider
cost/tokens
result
budget state
handoff reference
```

Checkpoint.

# 5. Phase B — codify measurable-goal Work lifecycle

Human direction from the merged workbook:

```text
measurable goal
→ execute
→ handoff
→ next measurable goal gets a new plan/fresh session
```

Current ACMS may still use multiple execution sessions inside one Work Item when needed.

## B1. Update architecture/requirements

Codify this **default policy** without removing flexibility:

```text
Feature / Program
  → Work Item = one measurable goal
      → one or more execution sessions if needed
      → complete handoff
  → next measurable goal = next child Work Item
```

A session crash, context rotation, or retry during the same goal does **not** automatically require a new Work Item.

If an ADR decision still needs human acceptance, keep it Proposed and make only additive/non-breaking changes.

## B2. Parent/child continuity

Inspect existing hierarchy first.

If not already supported, add the smallest safe representation needed to link sequential child Work Items to a parent Feature/Program.

Do not build a competing hierarchy.

## B3. Handoff → next work

Where existing Executive Agent authorization already permits it, support:

```text
completed Work + complete handoff
→ Executive Agent creates/proposes next child Work
→ same approved parent scope
→ provenance recorded
```

No silent scope expansion.

## B4. Review-loop guardrail documentation

Add an architecture rule:

```text
review findings = BLOCKING / REQUIRED / OPTIONAL
OPTIONAL findings do not prevent completion
review does not create new product scope
```

Do not yet invent a hard review-loop count unless repository requirements already define one.

Checkpoint.

---

# 6. Phase C — recovery learning and anti-infinite-loop behavior

The human has now given concrete runtime/work retry direction.

## C1. Runtime recovery

Policy:

```text
maximum 5 automatic recovery attempts
attempts should vary where possible
then Human Attention
```

Audit current ARM reconciler against this.

## C2. Persist recovery-method history

If missing, record at least:

```text
failure class/signature
attempt number
recovery method
timestamp
result
time-to-recovery
error
```

Prefer semantic/audit records over a second mutable history system if the existing event model is sufficient.

## C3. Strategy progression

Implement bounded strategy progression for failure classes actually supported by the current system.

Example only:

```text
observe/recheck
→ restart affected Bridge/harness
→ restart service/runtime
→ guest/VM restart
→ final safe recovery strategy
→ Human Attention
```

Do **not** automatically make PDU hard-cycle the fifth step.

## C4. Running without ACMS assignment

Human direction:

```text
do not automatically stop
```

Add/confirm an informational/warning semantic state:

```text
runtime RUNNING
primary ACMS assignment absent
```

No automatic remediation.

## C5. Work reassignment loop guard

Architecture direction:

```text
maximum 3 failed reassignments by Executive Agent
→ stop automatic reassignment
→ prominent Human Attention
```

Add data/audit support for:

```text
failed_work_count
reassignment_count
retry/reassignment reason
changed dimension
```

Do not automate the old subjective `<60% instruction match` rule.

Instead, document that failure should eventually be based on explicit acceptance criteria/evaluation.

Require a retry to record what changed, such as:

```text
different agent/model
revised instructions
additional context
different strategy
human clarification
```

Checkpoint.

If ACMS has duplicate authoritative sync state:

- ACMS may consume a summarized health signal if operationally useful;

Document ownership in architecture/contracts.

# 9. Phase F — Speed-to-Lead / external-agent architecture discovery

The human has made several important decisions but explicitly requested a separate architecture session.

Do **not** build the full product.

## F1. Record current decisions

```text

ACMS = internal orchestration infrastructure
external/customer Hermes starts sanitized/blank
internal company state must never be inherited
Speed-to-Lead is first external agent class
first portal persona = real-estate broker owner/operator
```

## F2. External agent bootstrap proposal

Create a proposal for a separate safe baseline, for example:

```text
agentifyme-external-agent-base
```

It may reuse safe generic mechanisms from `hermes-base-setup`:

```text
.gitignore strategy
secret scan
bootstrap/version metadata
state-sync mechanics
```

It must not copy:

```text
Startup Teams internal memories
internal skills
internal company instructions
internal secrets/configuration
```

Do not create the repo unless repository creation is within existing authorization; otherwise produce the proposed structure.

## F3. Explain/compare “Gateway” alternatives

Create:

```text
docs/architecture/EXTERNAL_AGENT_PRODUCT_BOUNDARY_OPTIONS.md
```

Explain that a service gateway is more than a firewall.

Compare:

```text
A. Product Backend/BFF → ACMS adapter
B. Dedicated External Agent Gateway
C. Edge API gateway + Speed-to-Lead product service + ACMS adapter
D. Public multi-tenant ACMS API (likely inconsistent with current direction)
```

Evaluate where to place:

```text
authentication
tenant identity/isolation
rate limits
spend limits
payload validation
tool allowlists
customer/client guardrails
auditing
A2A adaptation
```

Do not choose a security boundary merely because it is called a "gateway."

## F4. Threat/assumption analysis

The human identified the highest-risk technical assumption:

```text
external boundary actually isolates customers
and enforces adequate guardrails
```

Produce an evidence-oriented threat model/test plan covering at least:

```text
cross-tenant data access
credential misuse
tool outside allowlist
prompt/policy bypass
data exfiltration
rate/spend abuse
wrong-customer routing
external agent compromise
audit reconstruction
```

No penetration testing against production customer systems.

## F5. A2A placement

Evaluate where A2A belongs:

```text
ACMS ↔ Bridge/Agent
Gateway ↔ Bridge/Agent
Product Backend ↔ Gateway
```

Prefer standards-compatible boundaries but do not force A2A at every layer.

Checkpoint.

# 13. Credential rotation and branch protection

These remain useful but human-gated.

## Credential rotation

If explicitly authorized:

- execute the prepared runbook;
- never print secret values;
- update consumers atomically;
- verify new credentials;
- revoke old credentials;
- run end-to-end worker check.

If not authorized, leave as Human Attention.

## Branch protection

If explicitly authorized:

```text
PR required
required checks
enforce for admins
no routine machine bypass
document emergency path
```

If not authorized, leave unchanged.

For each code slice:

```text
branch
tests
PR
self-review
required checks

staging where applicable
production safe-release only under existing authorization
checkpoint/handoff
```

Do not use direct-to-main pushes.

Do not claim “passed” when not run.

Do not expose secrets.

No real PDU action.

No destructive agent/runtime cleanup without explicit scope.

Never re-introduce a single-file conf mount.\n- **`docker compose up -d <svc>` recreates depends_on services too** and can\n  bring the app back with the default image (`acms-app:local`). Pin\n  `ACMS_APP_IMAGE_TAG=<tag>` on EVERY compose up in tooling.\n- **Never command-substitute `compose build`** — its stdout is the build log\n  and pollutes captured variables (`invalid tag`). Send build output to\n  `>&2`; compute the tag, never capture it.\n- **pipefail + `grep -c` false positive**: `grep -c` exits 1 when the count\n  is 0; capture the count into a variable with `|| true`, compare numerically.\n- Recovery from stuck maintenance mode: `up -d --force-recreate\n  reverse-proxy`, then re-pin the app image, then verify via `/version`.\n\n## Bootstrap hop (chicken-and-egg first deploy)\n\nThe first deployment of the release tooling cannot use the tooling. Manual\nhop with identical guarantees: verified backup → stop app → checkout target\nSHA → build SHA-tagged image → migrate → start → validate → write release\nmetadata (mark `mode=manual-bootstrap`). Proven 2026-09-26 (~2 min window).\n\n## Verification hygiene (file-tool corruption lesson)\n\nGround truth for any file change is `git diff` / `py_compile` / `bash -n` —\nnever the tool result echo. Echoes can mislead in BOTH directions (phantom\nerrors and phantom success; this session had mid-write corruption injecting\ngarbage text). For large/critical writes: verify immediately after writing,\nand if corrupted, repair via a python rewrite with assertion pre-checks on\nknown anchor lines, then re-verify with git diff.\n\n## Pointers\n\n- `references/live-drill-2026-09-26.md` — bug-by-bug drill breakdown, exact\n  commands, recovery timeline.\n- `references/hermes-harness-live-tests-2026-09.md` — Hermes 0.17.0 live\n  control-mapping results (pause/resume unsupported, steer/interrupt verified),\n  scratch-profile setup recipe, bridge-to-ACMS wiring.\n- `references/hermes-worker-bringup.md` — the full worker-runtime bring-up recipe\n  (VM clone → Hermes venv install via qga → LLM gateway wiring incl. context caps →\n  api-server platform config shape → systemd unit → verification + traps) as\n  executed on acms-worker-001.\n- Repo docs: `deploy/README.md` (operator runbook), ADR-0009 + ADR-0010 (both\n  Accepted 2026-09-26/27), `docs/HARNESS_CONTROL_MAPPING.md`, `SPRINT.md`\n  (current slices), handoffs under `docs/handoffs/`.\n\n## Combined slice 3+4 state (deployed 2026-09-27, v0.6.0 @ 85f3c92)\n\n- **ADR-0009 Accepted** (amended: live evidence + explicit deployment-authority\n  policy); **ADR-0010 Accepted** — heartbeat 60s / STALE 300s / reconcile\n  3600s / fleet 86400s / alignment grace 120s / context warnings 70-85-95,\n  all config-backed via `ACMS_*` settings (settings.py gained the fields).\n- Human-readable keys `ACMS-WORK-000001` / `ACMS-ASG-000001` via the\n  `acms_key_counters` transactional counter table — never row counts, never\n  reused; allocated in `create_work_item` / `assign_primary`.\n- State dimensions stay separate (plan §5): connectivity is ACMS-derived\n  (UNKNOWN/HEALTHY/STALE/UNREACHABLE from contact recency), `agent_running`\n  is harness-reported tri-state — the UI shows both as independent facts.\n- Heartbeat = complete snapshot (`acms-heartbeat-v1`), unknown fields rejected\n  by the Pydantic model; semantic events only on material change (never\n  per-heartbeat spam); event sequences allocated via the same counter table.\n- Time-dependent tests: inject `now_fn` into TelemetryScheduler; watch for\n  tz-naive vs tz-aware datetimes when mixing `datetime.now(timezone.utc)` with\n  DB-loaded values (replace(tzinfo=utc) guard in the stale delta).\n- **Next steps logged in agents.md:** wire `ACMS_BRIDGE_TARGETS_JSON` into\n  CT122 env + dispatch/control buttons in the UI; slices 5–9 per master plan.\n\n## JINT-001 — Server Manager integration (live 2026-09-27, prod e1cd84ed, alembic 0005)\n\n- `acms/server_manager_client.py` (urllib, contract pinned Server Manager API 1.0.0) + `/api/v1/server-manager` routes: POST /agents (persistent identity reservation + `provisioning_requests` row with authority provenance per REV4 §14: human_in_acms | human_in_server_manager | pre-authorized_sprint_execution_context), POST /requests/{id}/provision (calls SM; ownership refusals → 403, SM errors → 502), GET /requests/{id} (correlates live job/runtime state). Migrations `0004_provisioning_requests`, `0005_prov_attempt`.\n- **State honesty (live-found bug):** the provision route MUST reflect the returned SM job state — SM job FAILED → request FAILED with the error, DONE → LIVE + runtime_id correlation via `list_runtimes`, else PROVISIONING. Unconditionally writing PROVISIONING stuck a failed request permanently (blocking both retry and truth). ACMS prod now runs this fix.\n- **Retry semantics (live-found):** same request_id replays the original (failed) SM job forever; deriving `|retry:N` by counting markers recomputes N=1 forever. Real fix = monotonic `attempt` column (migration 0005, default 1, increment per retry → `request_id|retry:N`).\n- **create_runtime timeout:** provisioning is synchronous server-side (clone+boot = minutes); the 30 s client default timed out while the job completed — ACMS then recorded FAILED for a LIVE runtime. `create_runtime` now uses timeout=600; other calls stay at the default.\n- **Honest-state correctness chained into worker bootstrap:** the request that hit the timeout recorded FAILED while VM 124 was actually LIVE — corrected manually via SQL (state='LIVE', runtime_id, job_id). When reconciling such rows, pull truth from the SM side (`GET /api/v1/agent-runtimes?acms_agent_id=`), never assume.\n- **Pitfalls found live:**\n  - **compose `environment:` enumerates explicitly** — new ACMS_* settings NEVER reach the container from `--env-file` alone; add passthrough lines to `deploy/compose.yaml` (live symptom: \"Server Manager integration not configured\" 503 despite env in /opt/acms/.env). # AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous agents working in ACMS.

## Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, define mandatory governance boundaries, and approve merges.

Agents may inspect the repository, plan, implement approved requirements, decompose approved scope into tasks, create tests, update affected documentation, create handoffs, add out-of-scope discoveries to `FUTURE_WORK.md`, draft ADRs/TDRs, and execute non-destructive work inside explicitly approved scope/budget/security/environment boundaries.

Agents must not silently add/remove product scope, promote future work, accept their own new significant ADR, merge their own feature work, exceed a budget, cross tenant/context boundaries, deploy to production, alter credentials/permissions, perform destructive infrastructure actions, or modify protected Organization/Product/Project/Feature context without required human authorization.

## Read before planning

For every meaningful change read:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/BUSINESS_PROBLEMS.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. relevant accepted/proposed ADRs
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope/follow-up work is involved
8. `INITIAL_IDEAS.md` when source provenance matters

## Requirement discipline

Implementation must trace to one or more `ACMS-REQ-###` entries. Before editing summarize the requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, files/components likely to change, assumptions, and validation.

If work is outside approved requirements, ask for human approval or record it in `FUTURE_WORK.md`.

## Future-work provenance

Every future-work item must identify one of:

- **Human-Directed**
- **Agent-Discovered**
- **Source-Derived**

Human-Directed does not mean approved; it remains future scope until promoted.

## Centralized governance, decentralized execution

ACMS controls approved assignment, scope, budget, context access, permissions, and governance. It must not become the synchronous permission service for every shell command/tool call/test/retry.

A worker agent may autonomously execute implementation details inside its approved envelope.

An agent may have:

- one primary ACMS work assignment; and
- zero or more registered background routines/cron jobs.

## Context authority

Protected context levels:

- Organization
- Product
- Project
- Feature

Agents may propose changes but may not modify these without human authorization or explicit delegated authority.

Agent, Task, and Live Session context may be modified within approved policy.

If approved scope appears wrong, execute it as far as reasonably possible, preserve state, create a handoff, and propose the alternate direction instead of silently changing scope.

## Handoffs

Meaningful work must produce/update a Markdown handoff recording:

1. assigned goal and requirement IDs;
2. what was attempted;
3. what actually changed;
4. what the agent believes the current state is;
5. validation and results;
6. blockers/assumptions/unresolved questions;
7. recommended next action.

## ADR policy

Human-explicit durable architecture decisions may be recorded as `Accepted`. Agent-originated significant decisions remain `Proposed` until human approval.

## Approval timeout policy

- Reversible in-scope decision: checkpoint/preserve state, use documented fallback, continue if safe.
- Consequential but deferable action: defer that action and continue unrelated work.
- Mandatory human gate: never infer approval from timeout; hold/deny the gated action and continue only unrelated authorized work.

## Security

- Never commit credentials/secrets/customer-sensitive data.
- External/customer agents are a separate trust domain.
- Internal Agent Bridge endpoints are private-network/VPN endpoints unless an accepted ADR says otherwise.
- Do not weaken authentication/authorization/audit to simplify implementation.

## Git workflow

The documentation bootstrap is human-authorized for direct import. After bootstrap, feature work must use a dedicated branch and PR.

Recommended branch: `type/ACMS-short-description`.

Recommended commit: `type(scope): short description (ACMS-REQ-###)` plus issue/ticket when available.

Every feature PR must list requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, validation, risks, ADR/TDR changes, future work, and reviewer focus. Agents do not self-merge.

UI/API layers call this service; budget checks are never\n6|  duplicated in several callers.\n7|- Gate BEFORE new cloud execution: budget execution-check verdict decides.\n8|  - below hard limit  → dispatch\n9|  - soft exceeded     → dispatch + retain the warning in the event trail\n10|  - hard exceeded     → no NEW dispatch; durable event + Attention item\n11|                        (EXECUTION_REJECTED); in-flight work untouched\n12|  - hard + override   → dispatch + audited override trail already exists\n13|- Unknown cost stays UNKNOWN — never a fabricated zero (the budget engine\n14|  itself never blocks on unknown cost; the dispatch path preserves the fact).\n15|- Idempotency: a client-supplied idempotency key (or a stable hash of the\n16|  work/assignment pair) maps to ONE execution task; retries return the\n17|  original task instead of dispatching twice.\n18|- Correlation recorded on the task + event: Work ID/key, assignment, bridge\n19|  run id, budget-check verdict (audit trail), external A2A task id.\n20|\"\"\"\n21|from __future__ import annotations\n22|\n23|import hashlib\n24|from typing import Any\n25|\n26|from sqlalchemy import select\n27|from sqlalchemy.ext.asyncio import AsyncSession\n28|\n29|from . import budget_service, work_service\n30|from .bridge import BridgeError, get_bridge_for_agent\n31|from .telemetry_service import add_event\n32|from .work_models import ExecutionTaskCreate, ExecutionTaskRecord, WorkItemRecord\n33|\n34|\n35|class DispatchError(Exception):\n36|    \"\"\"Machine-readable dispatch failure (message = stable error code).\"\"\"\n37|\n38|    def __init__(self, code: str, http_status: int = 409, detail: dict | None = None):\n39|        super().__init__(code)\n40|        self.code = code\n41|        self.http_status = http_status\n42|        self.detail = detail or {}\n43|\n44|\n45|def _idempotency_key(work_item_id: str, assignment_id: str | None,\n46|                     client_key: str | None) -> str:\n47|    \"\"\"Stable dedupe key. Client key wins; else the open assignment; else work.\"\"\"\n48|    basis = client_key or assignment_id or work_item_id\n49|    return hashlib.sha256(f\"acms-dispatch:{basis}\".encode()).hexdigest()[:32]\n50|\n51|\n52|async def _find_existing_task(db: AsyncSession, dedupe_key: str) -> Any | None:\n53|    \"\"\"The most recent task created from this dedupe key.\n54|\n55|    Tasks carry the dedupe key as external_task_id ``disp-<key>`` — the\n56|    ExecutionTaskRecord therefore doubles as the idempotency ledger without a\n57|    new table.\n58|    \"\"\"\n59|    rows = (await db.execute(\n60|        select(ExecutionTaskRecord)\n61|        .where(ExecutionTaskRecord.external_task_id == f\"disp-{dedupe_key}\")\n62|        .order_by(ExecutionTaskRecord.started_at.desc())\n63|        .limit(1)\n64|    )).scalars().all()\n65|    return rows[0] if rows else None\n66|\n67|\n68|async def dispatch_work(db: AsyncSession, *, work_item_id: str,\n69|                        instruction: str, agent_id: str | None = None,\n70|                        idempotency_key: str | None = None,\n71|                        session_id: str | None = None,\n72|                        actor: str = \"api\") -> dict[str, Any]:\n73|    \"\"\"Dispatch NEW cloud execution for a Work Item — the ONLY path that does.\n74|\n75|    Returns a dict:\n76|      dispatched: bool          — False when the gate blocked or dedupe hit\n77|      reason: str               — dispatched | hard_budget_exceeded | duplicate\n78|      task_id / external_task_id — correlation ids\n79|      budget_check: dict        — the verdict used for the gate (audit trail)\n80|    Raises DispatchError for structural failures (unknown work/agent/bridge).\n81|    \"\"\"\n82|    # ---- 1. resolve the work item (+ open assignment) ----------------------\n83|    work = await db.get(WorkItemRecord, work_item_id)\n84|    if work is None:\n85|        raise DispatchError(\"unknown-work-item\", 404)\n86|    assignments = await work_service.list_assignments(db, work_item_id=work_item_id)\n87|    active = next((a for a in assignments if a.status == \"ACTIVE\"), None)\n88|    if agent_id is None and active is None:\n89|        raise DispatchError(\"no-active-assignment\", 409)\n90|    target_agent = agent_id or active.agent_id\n91|    if active is not None and agent_id is not None and agent_id != active.agent_id:\n92|        raise DispatchError(\"agent-mismatch\", 409,\n93|                            {\"active_assignment_agent\": active.agent_id})\n94|\n95|    # ---- 2. idempotency: a retry must never double-dispatch ----------------\n96|    dedupe_key = _idempotency_key(work_item_id, active.assignment_id if active else None,\n97|                                  idempotency_key)\n98|    existing = await _find_existing_task(db, dedupe_key)\n99|    if existing is not None and existing.status == \"RUNNING\":\n100|        return {\"dispatched\": False, \"reason\": \"duplicate\",\n101|                \"task_id\": existing.task_id, \"external_task_id\": None,\n102|                \"budget_check\": None}\n103|\n104|    # ---- 3. budget gate (REQ-059) BEFORE any cloud execution ---------------\n105|    verdict = await budget_service.check_new_execution_allowed(db, work_item_id)\n106|    if not verdict.get(\"allowed\", True):\n107|        # hard exceeded without override → durable rejection event (Attention\n108|        # picks it up from the log; no separate mutable state) + hold dispatch.\n109|        await add_event(\n110|            db,\n111|            event_type=\"EXECUTION_REJECTED\",\n112|            actor_source=\"dispatch\",\n113|            agent_id=target_agent,\n114|            work_key=work.work_key,\n115|            assignment_key=active.assignment_key if active else None,\n116|            summary=(\"NEW cloud execution blocked: hard budget exceeded on \"\n117|                     f\"{work.work_key or work_item_id[:8]} \"\n118|                     f\"(state={verdict.get('budget_state')}; \"\n119|                     f\"override={'active' if (verdict.get('override') or {}).get('active') else 'none'})\")[:512],\n120|            metadata={\"budget_check\": verdict, \"dispatch_attempt\": True,\n121|                      \"work_item_id\": work_item_id, \"dedupe_key\": dedupe_key},\n122|        )\n123|        await db.commit()\n124|        return {\"dispatched\": False, \"reason\": \"hard_budget_exceeded\",\n125|                \"task_id\": None, \"external_task_id\": None,\n126|                \"budget_check\": verdict}\n127|\n128|    # ---- 4. resolve the bridge target --------------------------------------\n129|    bridge = get_bridge_for_agent(target_agent)\n130|    if bridge is None:\n131|        raise DispatchError(\"no-bridge-target\", 503, {\"agent_id\": target_agent})\n132|\n133|    # ---- 5. dispatch through the bridge (new A2A run) ----------------------\n134|    try:\n135|        result = bridge.send_work(\n136|            work_key=work.work_key or work_item_id,\n137|            assignment_key=(active.assignment_key if active else \"\") or \"\",\n138|            instruction=instruction,\n139|            session_id=session_id,\n140|        )\n141|    except BridgeError as e:\n142|        # dispatch itself failed — record it; budget NOT consumed (ACMS did not\n143|        # create a session), retry after the error is resolved.\n144|        await add_event(\n145|            db,\n146|            event_type=\"EXECUTION_FAILED\",\n147|            actor_source=\"dispatch\",\n148|            agent_id=target_agent,\n149|            work_key=work.work_key,\n150|            assignment_key=active.assignment_key if active else None,\n151|            summary=f\"A2A dispatch failed: {str(e)[:180]}\",\n152|            metadata={\"dedupe_key\": dedupe_key, \"budget_check\": verdict,\n153|                      \"work_item_id\": work_item_id},\n154|        )\n155|        await db.commit()\n156|        raise DispatchError(\"bridge-error\", 502, {\"detail\": str(e)[:300]}) from None\n157|\n158|    run_id = (result or {}).get(\"run_id\") or (result or {}).get(\"id\")\n159|\n160|    # ---- 6. record the execution task + correlation ------------------------\n161|    task, err = await work_service.create_execution_task(db, ExecutionTaskCreate(\n162|        work_item_id=work_item_id,\n163|        agent_id=target_agent,\n164|        external_task_id=f\"disp-{dedupe_key}\",\n165|    ))\n166|    if task is None:\n167|        raise DispatchError(err or \"task-record-failed\", 500)\n168|\n169|    # {"content": "1|\"\"\"A2A dispatch API — REV2 Phase C (budget-gated work delivery).\n2|\n3|The single machine endpoint for starting NEW cloud execution of a Work Item.\n4|Budget gating, idempotency, and correlation live in dispatch_service — this\n5|router is a thin adapter. Bearer-gated like the rest of the machine API.\n6|\"\"\"\n7|from __future__ import annotations\n8|\n9|from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, HTTPException, Request, status\n10|from pydantic import BaseModel, Field\n11|from sqlalchemy.ext.asyncio import AsyncSession\n12|\n13|from .db import get_session\n14|from .dispatch_service import DispatchError, dispatch_work\n15|from .security import require_admin_token\n16|\n17|router = APIRouter(prefix=\"/api/v1/dispatch\", dependencies=[Depends(require_admin_token)])\n18|\n19|\n20|class DispatchRequest(BaseModel):\n21|    instruction: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=20000)\n22|    agent_id: str | None = Field(default=None, max_length=36)\n23|    idempotency_key: str | None = Field(default=None, max_length=200)\n24|    session_id: str | None = Field(default=None, max_length=255)\n25|    actor: str | None = Field(default=None, max_length=128)\n26|\n27|\n28|class DispatchResponse(BaseModel):\n29|    dispatched: bool\n30|    reason: str\n31|    work_item_id: str\n32|    task_id: str | None = None\n33|    external_task_id: str | None = None\n34|    budget_check: dict | None = None\n35|\n36|\n37|@router.post(\"/work/{work_item_id}\", response_model=DispatchResponse)\n38|async def dispatch_work_item(work_item_id: str, body: DispatchRequest,\n39|                             request: Request,\n40|                             db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_session)) -> DispatchResponse:\n41|    \"\"\"Dispatch NEW cloud execution for a Work Item (REQ-059 gated).\n42|\n43|    - 200 with dispatched=false + reason=hard_budget_exceeded → gate held\n44|      (durable EXECUTION_REJECTED event emitted; bounded auto-recovery state machine (§12.3) not implemented\n\n### Phase 9 — LiteLLM local gateway ✅\n- [x] LiteLLM Proxy on VM114:4000, nginx-proxied at `/v1`; logical model `startupteams/general` load-balancing all 4 local backends\n- [x] Virtual keys (one per Hermes agent pattern), budgets proven (0-budget key → 429), revocation, spend/token accounting in PG\n- [x] `OPENAI_BASE_URL=http://10.0.20.108/v1` pattern ready for Hermes clients\n\n### Phase 10 — First Hermes pilot ◐ (mostly)\n- [x] Pilot virtual key `hermes-pilot-vm910` minted and E2E-verified through the gateway\n- [x] DNS: `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal` → 10.0.20.108 (via OPNsense unbound host override)\n- ⬜ **The actual pilot VM does not exist yet** (CT900 Hermes Golden Master clone — user directive #7)\n\n### Unplanned but done (user-directed fixes)\n- [x] MIAM-00112 rebuild: VM401 restored from snapshot after another agent destroyed it; retention tiers (raw 14–30 d, 1-min agg 12 mo, aggregates long)\n\n**Phase 16 — Advanced provider/policy engine**\n- [ ] Direct provider integrations + cost/quality/policy scoring (official APIs only)\n\n### 🧹 Operational hygiene (small, do with next work session)\n- [ ] Pin CT906 (.195) with a static DHCP mapping on OPNsense (it leases inside the .190–.250 pool)\n- [ ] PostgreSQL schema: plan §17.2 defines ~30 tables (hosts, gpus, deployments, gpu_samples, request_usage, benchmark_runs…); Coverage summary vs. plan phases\n\n| Plan phase | Status |\n|---|---|\n| 0 Preflight | ✅ done |\n| 1 Clone/bootstrap VM | ✅ done (fresh image, cleaner than clone) |\n| 2 App shell + LDAP + audit | ✅ done (v0.3.0, plus Keys/Deployments UI) |\n| 3 PostgreSQL | ✅ done (on 147 as planned; .116) |\n| 4 Four-host monitoring | ◐ basic health only; no continuous telemetry |\n| 5 GPU power + cost | ⬜ not started |\n| 6 Emporia adapter | ⬜ not started |\n| 7 Lifecycle + auto-recovery | ◐ service control done; VM control + recovery FSM missing |\n| 8 Benchmark engine | ⬜ not started |\n| 9 LiteLLM gateway | ✅ done (keys, budgets, 4-backend pool) |\n| 10 Hermes pilot | ◐ key minted; pilot VM (CT900 clone) not built; OpenRouter config UI pending |\n| 11 Routing/capacity mgmt | ⬜ not started |\n| 12 OpenRouter fallback | ⬜ blocked on key; caps mandatory first |\n| 13 RDMA qualification | ⬜ not started |\n| 14 Multi-node vLLM | ⬜ not started |\n| 15 Storage/full logs | ⬜ not started (metadata-only default already correct) |\n| 16 Provider/policy engine | ⬜ not started |\n\n**Roughly:** core control plane ~complete; **5 of 17 phases fully open**, 5 partial. The system works today as a local gateway + key authority + deployment console, but it does not yet measure power/cost, benchmark, queue, or qualify capacity — which are the plan's differentiators for responsible local-LLM operations.\n\n## 4. Executive objective\n\nCreate a dedicated VM named:\n\n```text\nMARION-IA-USA LLM Manager\n```\n\non `MIAM-00135`, initially bootstrapped from the known-good GPU Driver Installer controller VM architecture but fully re-identified and stripped of copied GDI secrets/state.\n\nThe LLM Manager will become the stable point through which local Hermes agents and other authorized internal clients use LLM inference. It should:\n\n- monitor and control vLLM deployments on `MIAM-00111`, `MIAM-00112`, `MIAM-00143`, and `MIAM-00144`\n- issue a distinct API key to each Hermes agent\n- expose a stable OpenAI-compatible endpoint\n- route requests to healthy local deployments first\n- wait a deliberate, configurable interval for local capacity instead of prematurely buying cloud inference\n- fall back to OpenRouter only when policy permits and local capacity/health criteria are not satisfied\n- enforce OpenRouter spend caps and per-agent policy\n- benchmark model/topology combinations and keep historical results\n- collect GPU, vLLM, request, health, and power telemetry\n- calculate energy cost and performance-per-dollar\n- use GPU onboard telemetry as the primary dynamic local power signal\n- ingest Emporia whole-circuit/PDU/mini-split data as an independent facility-level measurement source\n- store operational metadata and history in PostgreSQL\n- offload high-volume or full prompt/response history away from the constrained `MIAM-00135` system disk\n- provide controlled vLLM service lifecycle actions and bounded VM recovery actions\n- provide a future path for multi-node vLLM deployments using the 100 Gb interconnect between `MIAM-00111`, `MIAM-00143`, and `MIAM-00144`\n- eventually compare/rank local inference, OpenRouter, direct APIs, and other officially supported provider integrations by availability, quality, cost, performance, context, privacy, and policy\n\nThe LLM Manager is a **control plane and gateway**. It does not normally host model weights or perform GPU inference itself.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Decisions locked in v0.2\n\nThe following answers from Section 28 of v0.1 are now treated as design decisions.\n\n| Item | v0.2 decision |\n|---|---|\n| VM identity | Candidate `VMID 108`, candidate `10.0.20.108`; fail closed unless both are proven unused |\n| Gateway | LiteLLM Proxy plus a Startup Teams custom management/control web application |\n| Client keys | One distinct API key per Hermes agent |\n| Qwen 3.6 normal load | Design around about 10 concurrent agents/requests, with an initial hard concurrency envelope of about 20 pending benchmark qualification |\n| Routing default | Local first, deliberate wait/queue, OpenRouter fallback only if the key policy allows it |\n| Cloud policy | Default OpenRouter master/aggregate budget must have hard caps; individual keys may override fallback permissions within administrator limits |\n| VM control | Manager may control both vLLM service lifecycle and bounded inference-VM recovery/restart |\n| Power | GPU telemetry first; modeled CPU/RAM/platform overhead; Emporia used as independent whole-circuit/facility telemetry |\n| Electricity planning rate | Rate 400 base energy (`$0.09992/kWh` Sep-May; `$0.13398/kWh` Jun-Aug) + year-round Emporia-observed variable fees/riders adder of `$0.05830/kWh`; effective planning rates `$0.15822/kWh` Sep-May and `$0.19228/kWh` Jun-Aug (displayed as 15.82¢ and 19.23¢) |\n| Logging | Metadata-first initially; architecture must support full prompt/response logs later with off-host storage |\n| Admin auth | Existing LDAP/LLDAP identity system; local break-glass/bootstrap admin only if needed |\n| DNS | `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal` |\n| Database | PostgreSQL; application designed so PostgreSQL can reside on a future storage-oriented VM on `MIAM-00147` |\n| Fallback timing | Local requests are given meaningful time to queue/run; slow local TTFT alone is not a reason to immediately duplicate the request in OpenRouter |\n| Distributed inference | 100 Gb multi-node path is a first-class future capability but remains unroutable until independently qualified |\n\n### Important interpretation: API keys versus concurrency\n\nThe number of issued API keys should **not** be limited to 20. API keys identify clients; concurrency consumes model capacity.\n\nFor the known `Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ` deployment, start with a deployment profile such as:\n\n```yaml\nrecomm

Executive objective\n\nCreate a dedicated VM named:\n\n```text\nMARION-IA-USA LLM Manager\n```\n\non `MIAM-00135`, initially bootstrapped from the known-good GPU Driver Installer controller VM architecture but fully re-identified and stripped of copied GDI secrets/state.\n\nThe LLM Manager will become the stable point through which local Hermes agents and other authorized internal clients use LLM inference. It should:\n\n- monitor and control vLLM deployments on `MIAM-00111`, `MIAM-00112`, `MIAM-00143`, and `MIAM-00144`\n- issue a distinct API key to each Hermes agent\n- expose a stable OpenAI-compatible endpoint\n- route requests to healthy local deployments first\n- wait a deliberate, configurable interval for local capacity instead of prematurely buying cloud inference\n- fall back to OpenRouter only when policy permits and local capacity/health criteria are not satisfied\n- enforce OpenRouter spend caps and per-agent policy\n- benchmark model/topology combinations and keep historical results\n- collect GPU, vLLM, request, health, and power telemetry\n- calculate energy cost and performance-per-dollar\n- use GPU onboard telemetry as the primary dynamic local power signal\n- ingest Emporia whole-circuit/PDU/mini-split data as an independent facility-level measurement source\n- store operational metadata and history in PostgreSQL\n- offload high-volume or full prompt/response history away from the constrained `MIAM-00135` system disk\n- provide controlled vLLM service lifecycle actions and bounded VM recovery actions\n- provide a future path for multi-node vLLM deployments using the 100 Gb interconnect between `MIAM-00111`, `MIAM-00143`, and `MIAM-00144`\n- eventually compare/rank local inference, OpenRouter, direct APIs, and other officially supported provider integrations by availability, quality, cost, performance, context, privacy, and policy\n\nThe LLM Manager is a **control plane and gateway**. It does not normally host model weights or perform GPU inference itself.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Decisions locked in v0.2\n\nThe following answers from Section 28 of v0.1 are now treated as design decisions.\n\n| Item | v0.2 decision |\n|---|---|\n| VM identity | Candidate `VMID 108`, candidate `10.0.20.108`; fail closed unless both are proven unused |\n| Gateway | LiteLLM Proxy plus a Startup Teams custom management/control web application |\n| Client keys | One distinct API key per Hermes agent |\n| Qwen 3.6 normal load | Design around about 10 concurrent agents/requests, with an initial hard concurrency envelope of about 20 pending benchmark qualification |\n| Routing default | Local first, deliberate wait/queue, OpenRouter fallback only if the key policy allows it |\n| Cloud policy | Default OpenRouter master/aggregate budget must have hard caps; individual keys may override fallback permissions within administrator limits |\n| VM control | Manager may control both vLLM service lifecycle and bounded inference-VM recovery/restart |\n| Power | GPU telemetry first; modeled CPU/RAM/platform overhead; Emporia used as independent whole-circuit/facility telemetry |\n| Electricity planning rate | Rate 400 base energy (`$0.09992/kWh` Sep-May; `$0.13398/kWh` Jun-Aug) + year-round Emporia-observed variable fees/riders adder of `$0.05830/kWh`; effective planning rates `$0.15822/kWh` Sep-May and `$0.19228/kWh` Jun-Aug (displayed as 15.82¢ and 19.23¢) |\n| Logging | Metadata-first initially; architecture must support full prompt/response logs later with off-host storage |\n| Admin auth | Existing LDAP/LLDAP identity system; local break-glass/bootstrap admin only if needed |\n| DNS | `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal` |\n| Database | PostgreSQL; application designed so PostgreSQL can reside on a future storage-oriented VM on `MIAM-00147` |\n| Fallback timing | Local requests are given meaningful time to queue/run; slow local TTFT alone is not a reason to immediately duplicate the request in OpenRouter |\n| Distributed inference | 100 Gb multi-node path is a first-class future capability but remains unroutable until independently qualified |\n\n### Important interpretation: API keys versus concurrency\n\nThe number of issued API keys should **not** be limited to 20. API keys identify clients; concurrency consumes model capacity.\n\nFor the known `Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ` deployment, start with a deployment profile such as:\n\n```yaml\nrecommended_concurrency: 10\ninitial_hard_concurrency_limit: 20\nmax_issued_agent_keys: unlimited_by_model_capacity\n```\n\nThe concurrency values must be model/deployment specific. A different model, quantization, topology, context target, or GPU set may receive a different admission envelope.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Known inference baseline\n\n### 3.1 MIAM-00111 qualified baseline\n\nKnown qualified environment:\n\n```text\nHost:             MIAM-00111\nProxmox:          VE 9.2.10\nInference VM:     VM103 jordatech-vllm-test\nGuest:            Ubuntu 24.04.4 LTS\nKernel:           6.8.0-139-generic\nGPUs:             6 x RTX 3080 20 GB\nAggregate VRAM:   120 GB\nNVIDIA driver:    R580 open, module 580.173.02\nPython env:       /opt/vllm-venv\nAPI port:         8000\nModel:            QuantTrio/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ\n```\n\nWorking topology:\n\n```text\nTP1 / DP6 / EP6\n```\n\nCore flags:\n\n```text\n--tensor-parallel-size 1\n--data-parallel-size 6\n--enable-expert-parallel\n--expert-placement-strategy linear\n--quantization awq\n--dtype half\n--gpu-memory-utilization 0.92\n--kv-cache-dtype turboquant_k8v4\n--attention-backend TURBOQUANT\n--enable-prefix-caching\n--enable-chunked-prefill\n--scheduler-reserve-full-isl\n--watermark 0.05\n--kv-cache-metrics\n```\n\nValidated large-context operating point:\n\n```text\n10 concurrent requests\n229,376 input tokens per request\n+ 256 output tokens per request\n```\n\nDo not record the synthetic 250k test as a qualified model limit because tokenizer expansion exceeded the declared 262,144-token model maximum.\n\nRepresentative 4096-in/256-out throughput results:\n\n| Concurrency | Output tok/s | Total tok/s | Mean TTFT |\n|---:|---:|---:|---:|\n| 10 | 249.85 | 4259.49 | 4.27 s |\n| 16 | 271.21 | 4623.65 | 4.73 s |\n| 20 | 311.98 | 5318.69 | 5.20 s |\n| 32 | 319.69 | 5450.14 | 7.39 s |\n| 48 | 348.62 | 5943.41 | 11.13 s |\n\nFor normal Hermes service, concurrency 10 remains the initial preferred baseline even though aggregate throughput rises at higher concurrency.\n\n### 3.2 MIAM-00112\n\nKnown major difference:\n\n```text\n6 x RTX 5060 Ti 16 GB\nAMD Threadripper Pro 3955WX\n128 GB RAM\n```\n\nDo not copy the MIAM-00111 topology without qualification. Its 96 GB aggregate VRAM and different GPU architecture require a separate model-fit and benchmark envelope.\n\n### 3.3 MIAM-00143 and MIAM-00144\n\nThese are intended to reproduce or complement the MIAM-00111 inference stack, but each must independently inventory:\n\n```text\nGPU model/count/VRAM\nPCI BDFs\nIOMMU groups\nkernel\nNVIDIA driver/module packages\ninference VM resources\n100 Gb NICs/ports\nnetwork addresses\nRDMA capability\nGPU-to-NIC PCIe topology\n```\n\nNever copy PCI BDFs between hosts.\n\n---\n\n## 4. High-level architecture\n\n```text\n       Hermes agents / internal clients / LXCs / VMs\n                         |\n                         | HTTPS\n                         | per-agent LLM Manager key\n                         v\n       +-------------------------------------------+\n       | MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager                 |\n       | MIAM-00135 / candidate VM108              |\n       | llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal        |\n       +-------------------------------------------+\n          |             |               |\n          |             |               +------------------+\n          |             |                                  |\n          v             v                                  v\n   LiteLLM Gateway   Custom Admin UI OpenRouter\n```\n\nSeparate telemetry path:\n\n```text\nGPU NVML/nvidia-smi ----+\nvLLM /metrics ----------+----> LLM Manager collectors ----> PostgreSQL\nOS/VM health -----------+\nEmporia cloud ----------+----> Emporia adapter -----------> PostgreSQL\n```\n\nExperimental distributed inference path:\n\n```text\nMIAM-00111 <==== 100 Gb ====> MIAM-00143\n     ^                           |\n     |                           |\n   100 Gb                      100 Gb\n     |                           |\n     +======== MIAM-00144 <======+\n\n        multi-node vLLM / NCCL / RDMA qualification lane\n```\n\n`MIAM-00112` is not included in this ring unless a suitable high-speed connection is later documented and qualified.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Base VM and bootstrap strategy\n\n### 5.1 Candidate VM sizing\n\nStart with:\n\n```text\nVMID:             108 candidate only\nIP:               10.0.20.108 candidate only\nHostname:         marion-ia-usa-llm-manager\nDisplay name:     MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager\nOS:               Ubuntu Server 24.04 LTS\nvCPU:             8\nRAM:              16 GB\nSystem disk:      64 GB\nLocal data disk:  64-128 GB only if actually available/needed\nMachine:          q35\nFirmware:         OVMF\nQEMU agent:       enabled\nBallooning:       disabled\nAutostart:        enabled after acceptance test\nGPU passthrough:  none\n```\n\nBecause `MIAM-00135` has constrained storage, do not assume a large local telemetry/database disk. Keep all high-volume paths configurable and relocatable.\n\n### 5.2 Clone source\n\nUse the existing controller:\n\n```text\nVM107\ngpu-driver-installer-v4-1\n10.0.20.107:8443\n```\n\nas a **bootstrap image only**.\n\nRequired clone hygiene:\n\n1. Full-clone while source is in a consistent state.\n2. Keep clone network-isolated until identity reset is complete.\n3. Change VM display name and guest hostname.\n4. Regenerate machine-id.\n5. Regenerate SSH host keys.\n6. Remove copied application secrets, private keys, audit records, state, tokens, and TLS identity.\n7. Remove GDI application code/services not needed by LLM Manager.\n8. Retain only useful base hardening, QEMU guest agent, service patterns, reverse-proxy/TLS patterns, and OS configuration.\n9. Generate a new TLS certificate for `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal`.\n10. Run a secret scan before enabling the new network interface.\n\n### 5.3 Fail-closed VMID/IP preflight\n\nBefore any clone or network mutation, the deployment script must prove both identifiers are unused.\n\nVMID validation should include at least:\n\n```text\ncluster-wide Proxmox resource lookup for VMID 108\nMIAM-00135 qm list\n/etc/pve configuration search\n```\n\nIP validation should include at least:\n\n```text\nexisting Proxmox guest configuration search\nDNS lookup\nneighbor/ARP table lookup\nICMP probe\nARP duplicate-address detection from the correct VLAN\nDHCP/static reservation inspection where available\n```\n\nA failed probe does **not** prove an IP is unused. Any contradictory or ambiguous signal aborts deployment.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Software stack\n\n### 6.1 Gateway\n\nUse **LiteLLM Proxy** as the provider-normalization and virtual-key gateway.\n\nReasons:\n\n- OpenAI-compatible API surface\n- virtual keys\n- authentication/authorization hooks\n- spend tracking and budgets\n- rate limiting\n- provider normalization\n- retries/fallbacks\n- local OpenAI-compatible/vLLM endpoints\n- OpenRouter and future external provider adapters\n\nThe custom Startup Teams control application remains authoritative for infrastructure-specific logic such as vLLM lifecycle, GPU health, power, benchmark qualification, host maintenance, and MARION-specific routing eligibility.\n\n### 6.2 Custom control application\n\nRecommended:\n\n```text\nPython 3.12\nFastAPI\nUvicorn/Gunicorn\nSQLAlchemy\nAlembic\nPostgreSQL\nsystemd\nNginx or Caddy\n```\n\nSuggested layout:\n\n```text\n/opt/llm-manager/\n    app/\n    control/\n    collectors/\n    routing/\n    benchmark/\n    auth/\n    power/\n    providers/\n    distributed/\n    migrations/\n    static/\n    templates/\n\n/etc/llm-manager/\n    manager.yaml\n    nodes.yaml\n    models.yaml\n    routes.yaml\n    tls/\n    secrets/\n\n/var/lib/llm-manager/\n    cache/\n    benchmark-artifacts/\n    exports/\n    spool/\n\n/var/log/llm-manager/\n```\n\n### 6.3 Service separation\n\nUse separate systemd units where practical:\n\n```text\nllm-manager-web.service\nllm-manager-collector.service\nllm-manager-benchmark.service\nllm-manager-control.service\nlitellm.service\n```\n\nDo not let a hung benchmark worker take down the gateway.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Network, DNS, TLS, and access\n\nExpose one HTTPS name:\n\n```text\nhttps://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/\n```\n\nRecommended routing:\n\n```text\n/admin      -> custom admin UI\n/api        -> manager orchestration API\n/v1         -> LiteLLM/OpenAI-compatible API gateway\n/healthz    -> manager health\n```\n\nAccess policy:\n\n```text\nLAN:             allowed\nTailscale:       allowed where policy permits\nPublic Internet: denied\n```\n\nHermes clients should only require:\n\n```bash\nOPENAI_BASE_URL=https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/v1\nOPENAI_API_KEY=<agent-specific-key>\nOPENAI_MODEL=startupteams/general\n```\n\nThey should never require:\n\n- SSH access to inference servers\n- Proxmox credentials\n- vLLM host credentials\n- OpenRouter master credentials\n- direct provider master credentials\n\n---\n\n## 8. Admin authentication and authorization\n\nThe web UI should authenticate against the existing LDAP/LLDAP environment.\n\nRecommended roles:\n\n```text\nLLM-Viewer\nLLM-Operator\nLLM-Admin\nLLM-Security-Admin\n```\n\nExample permissions:\n\n| Capability | Viewer | Operator | Admin | Security Admin |\n|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|\n| View status/metrics | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |\n| Run benchmark | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |\n| Drain/undrain | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |\n| Restart vLLM service | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |\n| Restart inference VM | No | Restricted | Yes | Yes |\n| Edit deployment config | No | No | Yes | Yes |\n| Create/rotate agent keys | No | No | Yes | Yes |\n| Change provider master secrets | No | No | No | Yes |\n| Change global spend caps | No | No | Yes | Yes |\n\nA local bootstrap/break-glass administrator may exist for installation/recovery, but LDAP should be the routine administrative identity source.\n\n**Secret handling:** do not hardcode or reproduce the root password, LDAP bind password, Emporia password, OpenRouter key, or LiteLLM master key in this plan, source control, shell history, or generated configuration. Inject them through root-readable secret files or a later secret manager.\n\n---\n\n## 9. Hermes API-key and policy model\n\n### 9.1 One key per Hermes agent\n\nStructure:\n\n```text\nOrganization\n  -> Project/service\n      -> Hermes agent\n          -> one or more rotating API credentials\n```\n\nEach stored key record should include:\n\n```text\nkey_id\nkey_prefix\nhash\nagent_id\nproject_id\ncreated_at\nexpires_at optional\nenabled\nallowed_logical_models\nmax_concurrency\nrequests_per_minute optional\ntokens_per_minute optional\ndaily_token_budget optional\nmonthly_token_budget optional\ndaily_dollar_budget optional\nmonthly_dollar_budget optional\nlocal_allowed\ncloud_allowed\nfallback_policy\nprovider_allowlist optional\nlogging_policy\n```\n\nRaw keys are shown only at creation/rotation.\n\n### 9.2 Initial policy profiles\n\nSupport at least:\n\n```text\nLOCAL_ONLY\nLOCAL_FIRST_OPENROUTER_FALLBACK\nCLOUD_ALLOWED_EXPLICIT\nSPECIFIC_MODEL_ONLY\nCOST_CAPPED\nBENCHMARK_ONLY\n```\n\nRecommended default for normal Hermes agents:\n\n```text\nLOCAL_FIRST_OPENROUTER_FALLBACK\n```\n\nbut only after OpenRouter global caps are configured.\n\n### 9.3 OpenRouter budget controls\n\nBefore enabling fallback for any default/general key:\n\n```text\n[ ] global daily spend cap configured\n[ ] global monthly spend cap configured\n[ ] warning threshold configured\n[ ] hard-stop threshold configured\n[ ] per-project cap supported\n[ ] per-agent cap supported\n[ ] audit event generated on fallback\n[ ] actual OpenRouter reported cost stored when available\n```\n\nFallback should never silently convert an uncapped local workload into uncapped external spend.\n\n---\n\n## 10. Routing and admission policy\n\n### 10.1 Default sequence\n\nFor a normal general-purpose Hermes request:\n\n```text\n1. Resolve logical model and agent policy.\n2. Find healthy local deployments that satisfy context/capability constraints.\n3. Apply deployment admission limits.\n4. If local capacity is temporarily full, queue for the configured local patience interval.\n5. If admitted locally, let the local request continue even if TTFT is slow.\n6. ### [2026-09-29T07:39:05.992537+00:00] assistant

Found a **live production bug** (PR-worthy): `budget_service.py` writes `agent_id=""` on BUDGET_SET/OVERRIDE/CHECK events, which violates the FK on PostgreSQL (SQLite unit tests don't enforce FKs, so 142 tests passed while prod 500s). This is exactly the class of bug the plan wants caught. This blocks Phase A's budget step, so I'll fix it via PR. First, let me complete the chain diagnosis and check remaining event writers for the same hazard:

Priority 2 and 1 ideas remain in `FUTURE_WORK.md` until explicitly promoted.\n\n## Priority definition\n\n- **3 - Core:** required for the primary function of ACMS.\n- **2 - Needed for some features:** useful/important but not required for the first usable control plane.\n- **1 - Extra:** low-priority, optional, or experimental.\n\n---\n\n## Feature Set: Agent Registry, Identity, and Lifecycle Control\n\n### ACMS-REQ-001 - Persistent agent identity\n\n**Sprint Priority:** 3\n\n**Requirement**\n\nACMS shall maintain a persistent identity for every registered AI agent that remains stable across agent process restarts, task executions, sessions, model changes, and harness updates unless a human explicitly creates a new identity.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- Every registered agent has a unique ACMS agent ID.\n- Agent history remains associated with the same identity after restart.\n- Session/run identifiers are distinct from the persistent agent identity.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- Register an agent, execute work, restart its process, reconnect it, and verify the same ACMS agent ID and history are retained.\n\n### ACMS-REQ-002 - {"output": "### ACMS-REQ-055 - Work continuity across disposable execution sessions\n\n**Sprint Priority:** 1\n\n**Requirement**\n\nA Work Item and its Program shall survive across many disposable execution sessions. Ending or rotating an execution session must never end the Work.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- Work Item state is owned by ACMS, not by any single harness session.\n- Closing a session leaves the Work Item and its assignment active.\n- A new session can resume the same Work with a compact reconstructed context.\n\n**Provenance:** Human-Directed (AI–Human SOP, 2026-09-27; REV4-aligned).\n\n### ACMS-REQ-056 - Explicit execution Context Packages\n---\n### ACMS-REQ-057 - Handoff completeness before rotation\n\n**Sprint Priority:** 1\n\n**Requirement**\n\nSession rotation shall be gated on a verifiable handoff that covers Work ID, status, Git state, tests, deployment, decisions, debt, remaining work, next action, and artifact references.\n\n**Acceptance criteria**\n\n- A handoff-completeness validator exists and its result is recorded.\n- Rotation remains advisory until policy hardens, but completeness is always measurable.\n\n**Provenance:** Human-Directed (AI–Human SOP, 2026-09-27).\n\n### ACMS-REQ-058 - Economic execution-session telemetry", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

### [2026-09-29T07:51:33.944509+00:00] tool · tool: read_file

{"content": "1|# AgentifyMe Cloud Management System - Architecture\n2|\n3|This document records the current architecture intentions for ACMS. It follows the repository's lean arc42/C4 approach and uses Mermaid for diagrams.\n4|\n5|## 1. Introduction and Goals\n6|\n7|### Purpose\n8|\n9|ACMS is the control plane for orchestrating a fleet of registered AI agents. It provides durable work assignment, context governance, human steering, auditability, performance feedback, and cost/status visibility while allowing agents to execute autonomously inside approved boundaries.\n10|\n11|ACMS is not the agent-instantiation or model-serving system. LLM Manager owns agent instantiation/bootstrap, model/inference infrastructure, and authoritative inference/cost reporting.\n12|\n13|### Architecturally significant requirements\n14|\n15|- `ACMS-REQ-002` - orchestration without agent instantiation.\n16|- `ACMS-REQ-003` - standardized Agent Bridge.\n17|- `ACMS-REQ-009` - decentralized execution inside an approved envelope.\n18|- `ACMS-REQ-017` - hierarchical context model.\n19|- `ACMS-REQ-022` - internal/external context isolation.\n20|- `ACMS-REQ-026` - mandatory human approval boundaries.\n21|- `ACMS-REQ-031` - A2A-based communication profile.\n22|- `ACMS-REQ-040` - External Agent Gateway.\n23|- `ACMS-REQ-050` - avoid per-action centralized execution dependency.\n24|- `ACMS-REQ-051` - initial fleet scalability target.\n25|\n26|### Quality goals\n27|\n28|1. **Human control without approval fatigue.** Governance protects meaningful boundaries while routine reversible execution remains autonomous.\n29|2. **Interoperability.** Hermes, Pi, Codex, and future harnesses fit through one management contract.\n30|3. **Traceability.** Humans can reconstruct what was assigned, what happened, what the agent believed happened, and what artifacts resulted.\n31|4. **Security isolation.** Internal and external/customer trust domains do not mix by default.\n32|5. **Scalability by decentralization.** ACMS does not proxy every shell command/tool invocation.\n33|6. **Repeatability.** Agent identity/configuration and protected context are versioned and lockable.\n34|\n35|### Stakeholders\n36|\n37|| Stakeholder | Primary concern |\n38||---|---|\n39|| Startup Teams administrators | Reliable control of the AI workforce, cost, security, and audit. |\n40|| Project/product managers | Direction, scope, progress, blockers, human-attention items. |\n41|| Worker agents | Clear assignment, context, autonomy envelope, reliable control channel. |\n42|| Manager/Executive agents | Delegated authority, task decomposition, workforce coordination. |\n43|| LLM Manager | Clear ownership boundary and machine-readable policy/metric interface. |\n44|| Future AgentifyMe customers | Isolation of their agent/data from internal and other-customer context. |\n45|\n46|## 2. Architecture Constraints\n47|\n48|- Initial deployment is primarily MARION-IA-USA, but the architecture must not depend on a single location.\n49|- ACMS orchestrates but does not instantiate agents.\n50|- LLM Manager owns model/inference infrastructure and authoritative inference/cost metrics.\n51|", "total_lines": 331, "file_size": 16961, "truncated": true, "hint": "Use offset=51 to continue reading (showing 1-50 of 331 lines)", "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

[Subdirectory context discovered: work/llm-manager-project-framework/AGENTS.md]
# AGENTS.md — LLM Manager

These instructions apply to AI agents modifying the LLM Manager repository or MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager deployment.

## 1. Read before changing anything

Read, in order:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `FUTURE_WORK.md`
4. `SPRINT.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md`
7. `docs/OPERATIONS_AND_USAGE.md`
8. relevant ADRs/TDRs

Approved requirements define scope. Future work is not authorization to implement.

## 2. Service boundary

LLM Manager owns inference/runtime infrastructure concerns. ACMS owns work/project/context orchestration.

Do not move project requirements, Kanban/task state, persistent transcript history, human steering governance, or general ACMS context management into LLM Manager merely because an integration needs them.

## 3. Production safety rules

- Treat MARION-IA-USA as production infrastructure.
- Never replace a known-working model/runtime before capturing its exact rollback configuration.
- Put deployments into explicit maintenance before authorized manual work.
- Recovery has historically had more than one trigger; verify both desired service state and desired power/VM state before stopping a VM.
- Do not assume a stopped model stays stopped until recovery state is verified.
- Never start two VMs that share the same passthrough GPU devices.
- Avoid repeated rapid GPU-VM stop/start cycles on MIAM-00111; a known PCIe/AER/riser condition has previously required a host reboot to recover.
- Preserve custom/patched vLLM environments. Do not `pip install -U vllm` into a known-good runtime.
- Experimental model stacks belong in versioned paths/containers/VMs.

## 4. Configuration and model truth

The live registry and verified runtime state are authoritative for current deployments.

Do not infer that an alias is local merely from its name. Verify provider/runtime/model identity.

When documenting a model deployment, capture:

- actual artifact/revision/checksum;
- runtime/image/commit;
- quantization;
- host/VM/site;
- context;
- TP/PP/DP/EP and relevant runtime flags;
- RAM/VRAM/hardware;
- route/alias;
- health and benchmark evidence.

## 5. History and rollback

Deployment history must make engineering work reproducible.

- `Load Configuration` is non-destructive.
- `Restore & Serve` is a separate confirmed operation.
- Failed experiments may be retained but hidden by default.
- Do not delete the last known-good revision merely because a newer model works.

## 6. Benchmark discipline

A performance number without deployment context is misleading.

Link benchmarks to exact deployment revisions and record at least:

- TTFT;
- output tokens/sec per request/agent;
- aggregate output tokens/sec;
- concurrency;
- prompt/output token counts;
- tested context;
- RAM/VRAM;
- host/hardware/runtime;
- errors;
- representative Hermes/tool-use status when relevant.

When comparing models, prefer same-node tests. When comparing nodes, use the same model/configuration first so hardware effects are visible.

## 7. Secrets and data

Never commit:

- `.env` files containing secrets;
- API keys/tokens;
- passwords;
- SSH/private keys;
- production database row dumps;
- customer-sensitive data;
- raw logs containing credentials.

Sanitize configuration into `.example` files. Run a secret scan before commit. If a secret appears in a tool transcript or repository candidate, stop and surface it for rotation/removal.

## 8. Git workflow

- Work on a dedicated branch.
- Branch format: `type/ticket-short-description`.
- Commit format: `type(scope): short description (#issue-id)`.
- Do not invent an issue ID; create/use an approved issue according to repository policy.
- Keep capture/bootstrapping changes reviewable.
- A human must approve before merge.

## 9. ADR policy

If a human explicitly made a significant architecture decision, record it as an ADR and surface it in the PR.

If the agent thinks a significant new decision is required, draft `Status: Proposed` and stop for human approval before treating it as accepted.

## 10. TDR policy

Use a TDR for intentional shortcuts, patched runtimes, manual recovery procedures, missing automation, known hardware/software limitations, or other accepted debt.

## 11. Validation

Run the exact project validation commands once captured/documented. At minimum for a Python service, consider syntax/import checks, tests, config parsing, migration/schema verification, and API smoke tests in a safe environment.

Report `Pass`, `Fail`, or `Not run` accurately. Never infer a passing test.

## 12. Stop and ask for human judgment when

- requirements conflict;
- an ACMS-facing contract would be invented or materially changed;
- a destructive production action is required outside the approved maintenance scope;
- credentials/access are required and unavailable;
- a security boundary changes;
- production tests fail unexpectedly;
- a new persistent model policy would be made without approved authority;
- the diff expands beyond the approved capture/documentation scope.

## 13. Handoff standard

At completion provide:

1. requirements addressed;
2. source/code captured and source locations;
3. files excluded and why;
4. changes made;
5. validation commands/results;
6. production impact (if any);
7. ADR/TDR status;
8. future-work items discovered;
9. known risks/unresolved questions;
10. exact branch, commit, PR, and recommended next action.
